<!-- quote:pbu18-0134 -->
> my last period lasted march 20-25, was 2 days late but no positive pregnancy test before then. april 4th i started bleeding— like, heavy clots levels of bleeding for like 2-3 hours straight. i started panicking bc mid cycle bleeding has NEVER happened to me before, i start googling and had a list of things i thought it could be. i decided to take 3 pregnancy tests just to rule it out, and they all came back positive? 
> 
> i don’t bleed at all the 5th, spot a little the 6th after work, and then the 7th i bleed a bit more. 
> 
> the 7th i make a trip to urgent care to get it all checked out. the doctor pretty much tells me i’m wasting my time and money being there and that it’s impossible for me to be pregnant… i kept trying to tell her i KNOW it doesn’t make sense, that’s why i’m there. she just sends me home, no paperwork, no tests, doesn’t even grab my blood pressure. i go to another urgent care and this time they do a blood pregnancy test to test HCG levels, waiting on results to come back currently. 
> 
> all this to say— what the hell could this be??? i’m extremely confused :/ it feels nothing like my last miscarriage, the bleeding isn’t similar at all either.

<!-- quote:pbu18-0182 -->
> I’m newly pregnant after a MMC last year and a chemical this fall… I brought this up to my OB at my first appointment and I felt like she just brushed it off and did nothing to try and reassure me or set up some sort of plan going forward.

>

>

>

>

> 
> maybe this is mostly anger and fear but I’m upset they said all of that to me like that knowing my history and just being so  cold
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0228 -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0328 -->
> 09/10 I go to my scheduled procedure. I TOOK MISOPROSTOL as prescribed from the doctor. 2 the night before, two the morning of. Each pill was 200 mcg. I had some cramping and bled a bit after taking the pill. But no excessive bleeding where I needed a pad. 
> 
> I signed consent forms, getting ready for my procedure already took my clothes off from the waist down and all. The doctor comes back and let me know we can’t do the procedure. I’m super confused and when I ask her why, she said that I’m pregnant per my urine test. I FREAKED OUT . We were not trying, we’ve been together for years but had no kids in my mind. Honestly I was in shock but I was slowly grasping the reality and becoming attached to the idea of becoming a mom. My bf is happy too but obviously freaked out too. That weekend we told our parents and siblings and they were over the moon. 
> 
> On 9/18 they schedule me for my first sonogram but it was not with my same doctor nor office (same  women’s center but they have multiple locations). I wanted to schedule one asap cause i really wanted to see if this was real ( even though I bought 7 test and all came positive) and just really understand how far I am. Remember, August 20th I had a sonogram and there was no sac and no baby. So I really don’t know when I conceived.
> 
> So I have the sonogram and I keep asking to hear a heartbeat but the tech kept saying she’s just taking measurements and I have to wait for the doctor to speak with. I was annoyed cause I kept asking for anyone if I can hear a heartbeat but everyone kept avoiding me. Just to explain what I saw on the sonogram. I clearly see the sac, I see a little blueberry implanted on the left corner of the sac and pretty much that’s all I understood. But they did not explain to me and I’ve never been pregnant before to understand what am I seeing . They didn’t even print pictures for me. What I did see based on the measurements on the bottom it says 5 weeks and 6 days that’s all i understood. 
> 
> Same day, 09/18. They take my bf and I to another doctors office for our “follow up” mind you I’ve never been with this doctor before. First thing he asked me “did you do a sonogram? “I’m confused and say “umm yeah I just did?”?. 
> 
> He starts looking in his laptop scrolling through basically said they did not detect a heart beat and this happens to 15%-20% of pregnancies and it will be OK. I was immediately heartbroken and didn’t understand what’s next. My bf asked him two questions I didn’t think of. Did the misoprostol possibly cause this? And can this happen to women with PCOS. The doctor clearly said no cause it was a low dose (800mcg in total) and it’s no one’s fault. My bf believes the doctors won’t admit to the misoprostol cause that’s malpractice on their end. But as I didn’t know I was pregnant, they didn’t either. Basically he scheduled me to go back on 9/22 (4 days later) and if confirmed that there’s no heartbeat. Then Wednesday 9/23 have the D&C procedure, my worst nightmare.
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0329 -->
> Right now I’m OK just trying to stay calm and still take care of myself and just pray to God he knows what’s meant for me. After reading many Reddit post here made me hopeful but I wanted to share my story too.

> 
> Any advice or support would be greatly appreciated ❤️‍🩹

### 14. Medication decisions can be a conflict between fetal fear and the parent’s ability to function, made worse by contradictory authorities.

**Findings location:** §§2, 5–6, 8–9 and care navigation in §17.4. **Relationship:** Extends.

**Current conclusion:** “Care navigation includes trust and being believed.”

**What should change / remain:** Go beyond finding the right contact. Parents report warning labels that contradict prescriptions, OB and psychiatrist instructions that directly conflict, abrupt stopping and withdrawal, delayed ADHD treatment, self-started or self-adjusted treatment, refusal from fear, and relief after restarting. Partner blame can continue after clinician approval. Keep each reported effect and medical belief attributed; this digest does not adjudicate safety or give a medication plan.

**Evidence:** 47 quote records assigned here for navigation; 30 full fields below. These groupings are not mutually exclusive experiences or counts of people.

<!-- quote:pbu01-0077 -->
> i have been feeling horribly anxious. i have dealt with anxiety for my whole life and have developed a lot of coping mechanisms, such as swimming, yoga, working on creative projects, etc. i also have relied a lot on klonopin to help me through those days that feel relentless and i just can’t stop worrying and crying and feeling horrible. i obviously had to stop taking it for pregnancy and am proud of myself for making it this far (20 weeks) but am also feeling soooo done with the quality of life i am enduring right now. crying every day, worrying about everything. i so desperately want to take klonopin for some relief. the doctor gave me hydroxyzine as a replacement but it barely does anything , plus i am anxious even about taking that because the internet says it can be harmful to baby, even though my midwife prescribed it…?

> 
> Looking at the leaflet it says taking aspirin during first 6 months of pregnancy should only be if absolutely necessary. That it can cause problems in the baby like kidney problems, low amniotic fluid and narrowing of blood vessels in the baby's heart.
> 
> This seems very risky to me, given my last pregnancy.
>

>

I'd told the pharmacy it was for pregnancy and to match prescription, but off the shelf.

<!-- quote:pbu18-0139 -->
> My husband and I went to get our first ultrasound done the baby measured a week behind at 5 weeks and 4 days so we were unable to hear a heartbeat. During all of this I got my TSH labs drawn and I got the results yesterday… my TSH levels were way above the normal range it was at a 31.13. I’m now concerned because I have been feeling cold which is a big thing for when my thyroid is not where it should be. So after I seen that I doubled my dose of the levothyroxine to 100 (without doctor approval). I then get a call from my endocrinologist saying that I need to stop my prenatal and get labs again in 2 weeks. Nothing in my prenatal would have any effect to my TSH levels I made sure that it has no biotin in it. I then voiced back my concerns and said that where my level is makes me at a higher risk for miscarrying again and that at this point I just need that level to come back into the safe range since when I did research anything over 10 is saying to seek medical attention. She stated that she will consult with the other doctor who I will be seeing when I go next on their recommendations for me. Have any of you been through this or have any encouraging words it would mean a lot. Thank you in advance
<!-- /quote -->

> I’m 7 weeks pregnant with an unplanned pregnancy that I’m keeping.
> 
> I was on citalopram for about a year (20mg). As soon as I found out I was pregnant I stopped it cold turkey because I was terrified of harming the baby. I didn’t speak to a doctor first. That was about 6 days ago.
> 
> The depression and crying have come back hard. I feel unstable and exhausted. Ive also developed dizziness and  vertigo due to the withdrawal. I told my partner that me being this depressed and distressed might also not be good for the baby, and that I’m considering going back on the citalopram.
> 
> He is not supportive. He says I need to be strong and try to stay off it. When I was crying he said I’m already proving myself to be a bad mother because of how I’m being.
> 
> I don’t know what to do. Part of me wants the baby. Part of me is scared I’m making a mistake having a baby with someone who talks to me like that when I’m struggling. At first I was terrified that I was pregnant but as the weeks have gone by I already feel attached to the baby. 
>

### 15. Quitting, relapse, withdrawal fears and learning to live sober are part of pregnancy, not just retrospective exposure guilt.

**Findings location:** §§2, 6 and 9; new substance-use subsection. **Relationship:** Extends.

**Current conclusion:** “Care navigation includes trust and being believed.”

**What should change / remain:** Record the actual questions about cannabis, nicotine replacement, alcohol, nonalcoholic drinks, breastfeeding and exposure. Some fear quitting itself, some describe losing a coping mechanism, and one explicitly enjoys sobriety. Fear of disclosure belongs with the privacy pattern. Product mentions and proposed risks are evidence of what is being asked, not medical recommendations.

**Evidence:** 13 quote records assigned here for navigation; 13 full fields below. These groupings are not mutually exclusive experiences or counts of people.

### 16. Fear of birth can concern death, racialized risk, vomiting, transport and the survival of children already at home.

**Findings location:** §6 (birth fear), §9 and §10. **Relationship:** Extends.

**Current conclusion:** “Fear is also grounded in previous injury and how staff treated her; feeds are only one source”

**What should change / remain:** Retain the precise feared event and context. One Black author connects fear to her race and state; another worries about leaving two children without their mother; another fears nausea and panic despite therapy; another fears the hour-long drive because of previous pain-related bowel symptoms. A suggestion to stay off social media or remain flexible does not describe these mechanisms.

**Evidence:** 7 quote records assigned here for navigation; 7 full fields below. These groupings are not mutually exclusive experiences or counts of people.

<!-- quote:pbu01-0147 -->

I understand that the complications I fear are extremely rare, but reassurance hasn’t stopped the anxiety.

I’ve spoken to my midwife, but so far I’ve mainly been offered reassurance and calming techniques. Unfortunately, these haven’t been enough, and I feel I need more substantial professional support.

### 17. The same history of loss or trauma can support opposite birth preferences, and parents want those preferences explained and respected.

**Findings location:** §4 (preferences and consent), §§6, 9–10 and §17.4. **Relationship:** Confirms and extends.

**Current conclusion:** “Flexibility alone does not explain a good or difficult birth.”

**What should change / remain:** Set the accounts beside one another: induction can be what a bereaved body remembers and wants, while NICU loss can make early induction terrifying. A planned cesarean may protect against sexual-trauma triggers or feel safer after stillbirth. Include concrete consent, staffing, communication and holding/feeding requests, as well as practical barriers to obtaining the chosen procedure. Do not make one birth method the universal empowered choice.

**Evidence:** 43 quote records assigned here for navigation; 26 full fields below. These groupings are not mutually exclusive experiences or counts of people.

<!-- quote:pbu18-0033 -->
> I am 33 weeks pregnant now. I had a miscarriage in 2024 and then was diagnosed with having a uterine fibrous septum of 17mm (a septate uterus) almost reaching the cervix which was essentially a heart shaped uterus. I was born with this. I then had a successful hysteroscopic septoplasty surgery to remove the septum. My surgeon's notes said that my uterus after surgery was essentially like a normal uterus but it did have a 2mm divot at the uterine fundus (top part). My current Ob/gyn says this is not a normal uterus and that I could have risks in labor that need to be monitored. 
> 
> My doctor told me that if at the 2mm divot mark there is scar tissue then there is risk of uterine rupture during labor. Also since those tissues of the uterus for the longest time of my life did not experience normal contractions during periods she can't tell for sure how strong or weak they will be during labor. She was very keen on doing a planned c-section but I requested to get a trial at vaginal delivery. She is now ok with me laboring for 8 to 10 hours, however, she wants me to come to the hospital as soon as my contractions begin. Reading online I have realized this 8 to 10 hours is a very short window for first time labor especially if the time is counted from the first few contractions. Her reasoning (which I do understand) is that she wants to monitor my baby during labor right from the start. She told me that any point if there is fetal distress or any other warning sign she will go with a c-section. She has told me I won't be induced as that further increases the strength of contractions. She also advised against epidural because she is worried that it will mask any non contraction related pain that is an emergency until it is too late. 
<!-- /quote -->

> 
> *1.        Please explain each step of the procedure.*
> 
> *2.        Please introduce all medical team members and their role.*
> 
> *3.        Obtain consent for all non-emergency examinations, procedures, or interventions.*
> 
> *4.        Communicate openly with the mother and father during the procedure.*
> 
> *5.        Minimize unnecessary conversation and maintain a calm operating room.*
> 
> *6.        The father is to always remain with the mother.*
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0227 -->

> 
> *2.        Unless baby or mother’s health require otherwise, cord not to be clamped or cut until mother/father see cord is white and no longer pulsating.*
> 
> *3.        Father to cut umbilical cord, if medically safe and allowed.*
> 
> *4.        Place ECG leads away from the chest if possible, to allow mother immediate skin-to-skin.*
>

> 
> *7.        If mother is unable to hold baby, Father /nurse to help mother with skin-to-skin.*

> *8.        Support for breastfeeding immediately following birth.*
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0306 -->
>  I’m 38+6 FTM and I am feeling blindsided by my last OB apt. I’ve had a healthy pregnancy and do not have advanced maternal age. I go to a clinic that practices rotation, so you see all the drs at the practice to familiarize yourself with whoever may be on call during your delivery. I’ve liked all the providers and staff and until today, felt well enough informed throughout. I’m saying all that so it’s clear I feel positively about the practice overall. 
> 
> Well, today that maybe changed with one provider. She said that aside from my 39 week apt next Tuesday, I need an ultrasound that Friday (before my due date) and based on the imaging, they will schedule me for an induction at 40+3 or 40+5. That was news to me. I’ve been told that at 41 we will talk about induction methods and options that will be best at that time. I asked if there was a medical reason to bump up the timeline that I was unaware of. She told me the stillbirth rate doubles at 41 weeks and says “you wouldn’t want to be unsafe for your baby”, but when I asked her to quantify that rate she was unable to. So I am now sitting in an office being told by someone I trust that by waiting im taking a huge risk and I’m freaking out internally because all this is news to me. When I looked up the stat when I got home (I put it below for those who want to know) I was angry because I felt like her plan did not take my health into account and she was using fear to manipulate scheduling an induction. Why would she say that to a FTM without being able to quantify. And why change up the plan? My mom went late with my sister and I and we were both small babies. My pregnancy has looked a lot like hers and my baby is small (according to drs guess) and I just felt like the right way forward was to let baby cook as long as possible until they are ready or we need an intervention to get things going. Has anyone else been blindsided? What are the policies at your Dr for when to induce? Would this make you distrust your provider or am I being wild? Thanks in advance! 
> 
>   STAT Quantification:  the rate is 15 stillbirths at 40 weeks to 32 at 41 weeks / 10,000 births which is less than .5% chance and there are other factors at play including maternal health). 
<!-- /quote -->

### 18. Trauma can arise from disbelieved pain, failed relief, emergency treatment and separation even when the baby survives.

**Findings location:** §4 (birth and consent), §5 and §2’s recovery subsection. **Relationship:** Confirms and extends.

**Current conclusion:** “A healthy baby or an outwardly successful birth does not settle what the mother experienced; relief and improvement also appear.”

**What should change / remain:** Add the specific sequence: pain discounted because it does not register on a monitor, being told maternal pain no longer matters, escalating interventions, delayed relief, hemorrhage, surgery and a newborn transferred elsewhere. Preparation can fail because the body becomes incapacitated. Preserve later treatment, recovery and supportive clinicians beside trauma.

**Evidence:** 19 quote records assigned here for navigation; 19 full fields below. These groupings are not mutually exclusive experiences or counts of people.

<!-- quote:pbu01-0170 -->
> It started the day I went into labor. I have (had?) diagnosed tokophobia. Labor was a HUGE deal for me. I spent months in therapy to cope with the inevitable. And then when it happened, I discovered I COULD do it and I was so proud of myself. I found out in L&D triage that I had labored to 5cm on my own. That was amazing to me as I had previously thought that by the first contraction, I’d be out the door on my way to an epidural. So the fact I made it that far with no interventions really felt good. But then it was discovered my baby was breech. They said they would try to flip her. I agreed. Well, before they could even start, my baby’s heart dropped to 40. They waited it out for a moment and it went even lower. So we were taken to surgery immediately. I was so, so sad. I thought for sure I was going to labor naturally as the beginning went SO well? I know that sounds selfish. I’m so thankful my baby is okay. But it was a difficult labor situation to accept.
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0043 -->
> Hello, I had my child at 35 weeks, 0 days gestation 4 weeks ago and am looking for opinions/answers.   
> I lost my mucous plug at 31 weeks. They looked at my cervix with a speculum (no cervical check) and said it looked fully closed.   
> When I was 33.5 weeks I went into 4 days of intense prodromal labor. I went to L&D the first day of the prodromal labor and was 1.5 cm dilated and 50% effaced. I had consistent contractions around 5mins apart that then started to space out. They checked me 2 hours later and I had no cervical change so they sent me home. The next 4 days I had mild to moderate contractions, many that I had to stop and breathe through but could still talk a bit.   
> After that, I continued to have contractions intermittently, sometimes regular that were painful and had continued lower back pain. I talked with my OB about this and they gave me the steroid shots just to be safe and took me off work.   
> Then at 34weeks and 6 days just before midnight my water broke completely, and rushed out onto the couch I was laying on. I had only had one big painful contraction that day that was 2 hours prior.   
> I arrived at the hospital about 45 minutes later and before they could check me, baby’s heart rate dropped bad and they nearly rushed me to C section. Once then did check me I was 3 cm dilated. Then 2 hours later I was 4.5 cm dilated and 90% effaced. I had no contractions this entire time, just the same lower back pain I had been having. I was rushed to an emergency c section due to fetal distress soon after so I was not checked again.   
> My entire pregnancy baby was sitting very low in my pelvis, even during my anatomy scan. On the anatomy scan my cervix was 35mm and normal. Everything scanned normal. 
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0044 -->
> My question is, should I be scared to get pregnant again? This whole experience has made me terrified of preterm labor. I have felt like I was going to have a preterm baby the whole time. My baby felt too low and I was always told he was very low. Is my cervix incompetent? Why did this happen to me? Doctors seem to be unable to answer my questions and I just want some opinions, I am feeling very lost. Thank you.

<!-- quote:pbu18-0047 -->
> Hi all, I 31f had quite a traumatic first pregnancy, with multiple health scares, even so much as the doctor telling me I was going to birth a stillborn at the time of my induction. Had multiple hospital visits, unsupportive family and friends , and even finding out my husband cheated on me during all this.

<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0106 -->
> \- epidural stopped working when I was 10cm dilated 
>

>

> \- Allergic reaction to stitches

> This was also my first time in a hospital and I had a very difficult time accepting I had been “cut” and had a scar to deal with. Following the delivery I felt immense pain whenever I stood up which was explained to me as internal bruising from the forceps. I was still in pain and discomfort by week 4 which prompted my doctor to examine me and confirm I had an allergy to the stitches. In addition I had this feeling of having a misplaced tampon every time I walked: I panicked and convinced myself I had prolapse (which my doctor checked and said I did not). Turns out it was just the feeling of the internal stitches combined with overall dryness down there.
> 
> I really thought I would have a horrible, painful scar and only dared to look at it on week 6 and to my surprise I could barely see a faint line! I was prescribed a cream for the allergy and an oestrogen cream for the dryness. A wonderful midwife manually removed some of the stitches that hadn’t dissolved by week 3 which brought me a lot of relief from the feeling of tightness and having something “pulling”.
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0107 -->

<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0174 -->
> So I’m wondering if anyone has had the same experience as me. I F(26) had my son last July and he’s 10 months now. I got induced at 38 weeks due to preeclampsia. They tried so many ways to induce labor and I ended up C-section. During one of the things they tried- they used cervadil. I did fine before I had that- cervical checks were uncomfortable but not terrible. After they took out the cervadil and gave me a cervical check, I legit screamed and cried and begged the dr to stop. Every type of induction method after (foley balloon) was so painful. Probably the most painful thing I’ve ever experienced in my life and I’ve had a ruptured ectopic. To this day the thought of having sex/having sex gives me almost like ptsd and I tense up and get really bad anxiety. I only use pads now and not tampons. I’m just wondering if anyone had the experience with cervadil. They did the foley balloon after and they had to give me dilaudid. It was hands down the worst pain I had ever felt in my life :(

<!-- quote:pbu18-0221 -->
> So I delivered my baby 3 weeks ago and it’s taken 3 weeks for me to be comfortable enough to share this delivery story. I’m not sharing this to scare anyone but to encourage others to listen to their body and share what you’re feeling with your doctors. My OB practice has pregnant people on doctor rotation so you met all the OBs and can feel comfortable with them all. I had been telling them all for the last 3 months of my pregnancy something was wrong and I was scared of delivery ( my second child). I knew what to expect and I shouldn’t have felt as scared or anxious as I was. I carried to term 40 plus 1 but had been having steady contractions since 34 weeks, I for some reason can progress labor till they break my water, I had been 4-5 cm dilated since 34 weeks. When my water broken it was clear then I passed two huge blood clots and was told I had a silent placental abruption and now me and baby needed to be monitored extra. If his heart rate decreased and didn’t come back up or I started to bleed too much before the pushing phase I would be getting a c section and it wouldn’t be optional. I was lucky and delivery progressed fast, 6 cm dilated to pushing in 30 minutes, pushed for 5 minutes total. I was unmediated because my epidural didn’t work until 15 minutes after my baby was delivered. His shoulder got stuck on my pelvic bone and I got elbowed to force him out. The doctor hit the panic button while I was pushing as I started bleeding way too much. And I felt all of the affects of that. I was terrified and I still have been scared since. Long story short, if you feel off, scared, or nervous or something feels wrong please tell your doctors, my OB took it very seriously and had a note in my chart so all the OBs knew to be extra cautious. And thank the universe she did because I believe it saved my life because they caught something that didn’t show in any test or sono. 
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0222 -->
> I do have a therapist and she’s been a huge help in working through the feelings left behind from this. Thank you for the kind words and support. I’m hoping my story can help encourage others to advocating for themselves or their partners in a time of fear and joy.
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0232 -->
> My uncomplicated full-term pregnancy transformed to a traumatic birth within a few hours. We're talking failed epidural, defunct CTG and fetal electrode, oxytocin and vacuum-assisted birth with episiotomy that resulted in several serious tears, 3L haemorrhage, ICU stay, permanent catheter for a month, retained placenta, several revision surgeries, the works! There were no signs that my birth was going to go to hell, it just happened. During that experience and the post partum I endured, I tried a series of recommendations that are often recommended on this sub, from the hospital staff, popular OBGYN youtubers and the like. Here are all the things that failed, and the alternatives I found that actually worked for my Hard Mode birth and PP. I highly recommend everyone to prepare for the worst, even though *odds are you will be much better off than me!*
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0236 -->
> **FAIL:** Labour at home until contractions are close together  
> ***why:*** I went in while my contractions were still ages apart. After a 10m car ride, I was in a wheelchair from the parking lot to the ward. I still wasn't dilated much, but my pain was such that if I had waited any longer I would have had to call an ambulance to get to the hospital, because being upright was impossible.  
> **FIX:** Go by your pain and physical wellbeing, not just your contractions. You may have ages left of your labour, but serious pain can be debilitating to the point that you're immobilized. If they stick you in the waiting room, that's still a safer place to suffer than on your bathroom floor.
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0237 -->
> **FAIL:** Movement during labour, gas, TENS, etc.  
> ***why:*** Intense back labour made any kind of movement impossible, and no alternative pain relief so much as touched my pain. Note that if you have pain relief you may be able to move or at least change positions.  
> **FIX:** Lying on my side in bed, in fetal position, all the way to the pushing phase. Squeezing my husbands hand during contractions. These things obviously didn't remove the pain, but they helped.
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu19-0023 -->
> Since then, it’s been 3-4 hours later, my contractions have kinda gone away, and because new patients are coming in full term, they get priority to a bed. I’m just so tired and want to go home. I feel so guilty that I’ve caused this to happen and baby girl won’t come out healthy. I can’t stop crying. Plus, I miss my son. He’s my comfort and it’s the first time he’s spending the night without mommy or daddy.
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu19-0046 -->

She can't be touched now, but we hope soon. I hope to be able to visit her as soon as possible, but of course it is a big operation and I understand.

### 19. The first weeks may be spent injured or readmitted in hospital, physically unable to hold a baby or lift an older child.

**Findings location:** §2 (recovery), §§4–6 and §10’s first-weeks plan. **Relationship:** Confirms and extends.

**Current conclusion:** “Recovery is not just a supplies checklist”

**What should change / remain:** Build the account around actual capacity and location: infection, embolism, anemia, ongoing bleeding, inaccessible cribs, painful holding, NICU separation and the inability to reach appointments. A willing partner may still be unable to care for both an injured adult and a newborn. Later retrospective accounts describe consequences beyond two weeks; do not relabel their timing to fit the product window.

**Evidence:** 24 quote records assigned here for navigation; 24 full fields below. These groupings are not mutually exclusive experiences or counts of people.

<!-- quote:pbu01-0067 -->
> I’m 6 days postpartum, and I have been feeling this tightness in my chest (feels like something is sitting on the middle of my chest, though not super heavy) while at rest since 3 days postpartum. It usually manifests when I’m laying down flat. I ignored it for 2 days, thinking it might just be my body adjusting back to not being pregnant anymore. 
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu01-0068 -->
> However, since my discharge summary from L&D says to go to ED if I have shortness of breath or chest tightness/pain, plus the answers I get from google, I decided to go in.
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu01-0069 -->
> The tightness never went away. My d-dimer is elevated, and I was diagnosed with pulmonary embolism. I’m currently sitting here in ED with the same intensity of chest tightness while waiting for my blood thinner shot, thanking God and my gut for listening to my body, and for coming in to get checked. I do have to give myself a shot daily for 3 months, but I’m so so so thankful I got myself checked.
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu01-0117 -->
> Background - I have a 3 week old and prior to my c section, when my eldest would wake up, I’d pick her up and she would 99% of the time settle right away in my arms. I then obviously went to hospital and this behaviour stopped. Now when she has occasionally done it, we’ve popped her in our bed but her being NEXT to me doesn’t do much. However obviously with a c section I couldn’t hold her. Last night I decided screw if, and after screaming for 20 mins I picked her up and she calmed down in my arms. 
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0072 -->
> Thank youuuu. Finally an actually helpful response. Girlll I did not expect to feel as train wrecked as I do 😒😒😒 I hear you on the complications I’ve had complication after complication and I was a very healthy person before I had a baby. I still went to the gym at 40 weeks pregnant ☹️ I hope you recover soon 🫶🏻🫶🏻🫶🏻
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0238 -->
> **FAIL:** Padsicles or witch hazel.  
> ***why:*** Cold compresses were discouraged by the hospital due to the chance of triggering a UTI. Witch hazel did absolutely nothing for my pain.  
> **FIX:** Lidocaine gel. Liberally applied directly to the skin and wounds every few hours, and a big pool of it into the otherwise warm and dry pad.
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0239 -->
> **FAIL:** Diapers.  
> ***why:*** The hospital gauged PP bleeding in maxi-pads filled per hour(s).  
> **FIX:** Biggest pads I could find, without gel, scent or other nonsense, plus boxer-style panties that were 1 size too big.
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0241 -->
> **FAIL:** Washable bra inserts, cloth nappies (for infants).  
> ***why:*** The volume of these that I went through would have required me to buy more than would ever have been cost-efficient, and it just wasn't practical.  
> **FIX:** Disposables. If you're interested in eco-friendly alternatives then re-evaluate the situation after birth, perhaps after 3 or so months.
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0242 -->
> **FAIL:** Co-sleeping, traditional crib.  
> ***why:*** Due to anaemia, medication and my injuries, there was absolutely no way for me to co-sleep with the baby, nor carry the child to a crib, nor even bend over slightly. I was physically reduced for a full year after birth.  
> **FIX:** Bedside crib with wall that could be pulled all the way up and all the way down. I nursed the baby in bed, then sort of shuffled him over into the crib and pulled the wall all the way back up to ensure I didn't move myself or any bedding into his space while I slept. When the child could pull or push himself up slightly, we transitioned to a floor bed (adult-size, to easily nurse or cuddle the child to sleep) with a low rail, skipping the regular crib entirely.
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0243 -->
> **FAIL:** Nests, crib wall paddings, baby duvets, etc.  
> ***why:*** Baby could easily shuffle and move in bed from birth. He would frequently wind up with his face against a wall. It was impossible for me and my husband to keep track of his movements during the night; we both basically blacked out when asleep.  
> **FIX:** Sleeping bag/sack for baby, crib made with naked wood rails and a firm mattress only.  Owlet sock also helped us feel a little safer.
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0244 -->
> **FAIL:** Relying on husband and myself only.  
> ***why:*** Taking care of a newborn is already a big deal. It was physically impossible for my husband to look after both of us 100% of the time, much as he tried (and he is a strong man, physically and mentally). Husband also discovered he sleep-walks when severely sleep deprived, so he wasn't a trustworthy helper during the night. I actually had to keep him from changing nappies as a sleepwalker, because he would do it automatically without actually waking up.  
> **FIX:** Calling on everyone we knew to help out with things big and small, and setting up the "nursery" for ease of use NOT aesthetics. We didn't have a village exactly, but we had a patchwork of people who would individually help out once or twice, which combined made a big difference. Groceries we ordered delivered to the door, without exception. This time around, I've asked people in advance to help out around my due date, and I've set up the master bedroom to have the bedside crib, nappy station, pump and everything else I need. No nursery, just everything within minimum distance of my bed.
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0245 -->
> **FAIL:** Wipeable changing pads (also, any strategy to encourage baby to finish their business before you clean up)  
> ***why:*** I went through 3 wipeable mats, from low to high-end, and they all bubbled or showed signs of mould after frequent washing. I tried castile soap, chlorine and ethanol solutions, all of which damaged the pads over time. The only other option was wiping with just water, and that wasn't sanitary enough for me. Baby will pee whenever they feel like it, and shoot poop across impressive distances just as you're pulling out a fresh nappy.  
> **FIX:** Pad with changeable cover, and a thick guest towel under the butt area. For big spills, the whole thing can be changed and the mattress wiped, while the towels can be changed as often as you like without having to wash the entire cover. We just bought a lot of cheap guest towels instead of several additional covers. In the beginning, when baby is very messy, you can also use disposable changing mats.
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0246 -->
> \*\*FAIL:\*\* "Sleep while you can."  
> ***why:*** I was never able to sleep during the day, nor was it practical. During the day, baby would only ever sleep while being held.  
> **FIX:** Cope with the sleep you get. During the day, relax as much as possible. It gets easier. If you're still struggling with night-wakings when your child grows older, sleeping on a mattress beside their (floor-)bed makes nights much easier. You can take turns with your partner. Also, try to schedule your social calls, brainrot, intimacy and hobbies during the day, so you can focus on just sleep in the evening, and get less stressed out if your child stays up longer than you'd like.
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0247 -->
> **FAIL:** PP belly belts/binders.  
> ***why:*** Wore these for months and I still developed diastasis recti.  
> **FIX:** Surgery. I noticed an improvement in *appearance* from simply walking with good posture (tight abs and butt, straight back) and regular cycling, but it ultimately made no measurable difference on a CT. Scheduled for surgery when I've decided on no more kids.
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0250 -->

### 20. Feeding choices can trade off milk supply, pain, medication, sleep, bonding and the ability to function.

**Findings location:** §2 (feeding/recovery), §§4–6 and §10. **Relationship:** Confirms and extends.

**Current conclusion:** “The first weeks include physical incapacity, feeding pressure, conflicting instructions, loneliness, regret and delayed connection.”

**What should change / remain:** Distinguish the parent who wants to maintain breastfeeding from the parent relieved to stop pumping. A good latch or abundant milk can coexist with burdensome pumping from ICU; a screening result can make successful feeding feel harmful. Others attribute supply loss to repeated weighing and incomplete instruction. Formula is relief in some accounts and grief in others. These are reported sequences, not verified treatment effects.

**Evidence:** 15 quote records assigned here for navigation; 15 full fields below. These groupings are not mutually exclusive experiences or counts of people.

<!-- quote:pbu01-0171 -->

My pregnancies are super healthy and healthy vaginal birth second time no stitching or tearing, I'm young early twenties so age is not a factor either to this problem, I did breastfeed my first through my pregnancy but that soon didn't work cause midway my milk dried up and then I offered comfort feeds for a few minutes per day maximum 10 mins up until the day I gave birth, then I switched right to feeding my newborn and my milk came in. 
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0097 -->
> I got a call today from the company that did my baby’s newborn screening saying his levels were abnormal on his screening for galactocemia. It’s a rare metabolic disorder where he wouldn’t be able to break down the sugar in all forms of milk, including breast milk. if left untreated, it can cause seizures, liver failure and even death. Obviously i am absolutely in shambles and freaking out. He has been exclusively breast fed since birth. S/S were jaundice, poor feeding, failure to thrive, lethargy, diarrhea, vomiting, and excessive fussiness. 
> 
> In the hospital, he did lose about 7% of his body weight, but by the time he had his first pediatrician visit the next day he had gained a considerable amount back. No concernable jaundice. He has a crazy appetite and was feeding pretty often and drinking great amounts of breast milk with no issue, he hasn’t vomited or been fussy at all. I was 100% taken by surprise as we thought he was doing fantastic, now i’m worried that I have been feeding him and making him sick. it’s breaking my heart into a million pieces. 
> 
> I was told to switch to soy-based formula for now, he has an appointment Friday to be retested and i’ll be an absolute mess until we know for sure. 
> 
> Does anyone have any experience with something like this?? i’m so lost and scared for him. 
> 
> I want to keep pumping to maintain my supply in case he can go back to taking breast milk but at the same time, if he can’t have it i’d rather let my supply start to go.. 
> 
> The test apparently has a higher chance of false positives so im trying to keep my hopes up. I don’t know what further testing needs to be done or what to expect moving forward. 
> 
> any advice is GREATLY appreciated 🥺
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0105 -->
> I've been off my adderall for about 11 months now. I did not realize how poor my mental health had gotten during pregnancy  and after welcoming my LO into the world. I was combo feeding up until two weeks ago but stopped pumpimg due to under supply and LO's reflux. Today I went back on my adderall.  And the difference is amazing. I was getting paranoid, more fatigued than usual, and short tempered. No depression, no increase in anxiety. The anxiety was already there. I was having scary intrusive thoughts about the health and wellness of my baby. Today, reduced anxiety, no intuitive thoughts, no paranoia,  and my fatigue level feels manageable.
> 
>  
> I wish  I had stopped pumping earlier. I wanted to give my LO the best start to life I could give him. I didn't realize taking care of myself was a part of that. I did not want to give up pumping, but giving it up  and getting back on my pre pregnancy meds was a missing peice in the taking care of myself puzzle.
> 
>  I have more patience and so, so much less frustration. I have the motivation to read to him more and to interact with him more. I had gotten to the point of being so tired I was staring into space while holding him and not interacting as much as I wanted to with him.
> 
> That's all I have to say on the subject, I think. I hope everyone is trying to find the time to care for themselves. I know it's hard with a newborn. Try and alow yourselves some grace. 
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0113 -->
> When my daughter was born, my plan was to exclusively breastfeed. But at her two-day appointment, the pediatrician scared me into supplementing with formula because her post-birth weight loss was at the “lower end of the normal spectrum”— even though my milk supply was totally fine. Since I started supplementing with formula, my milk supply dropped drastically even with pumping, and she’s now almost exclusively getting formula. It breaks my heart. It makes me so angry that I let myself be talked out of what I wanted just because I was a scared new mom, and I get frustrated to the point of tears every single day because I can’t get my supply back up (I’m now 3 months postpartum).
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0114 -->
> I also feel like a horrible mom because I can’t seem to get my supply up to breastfeed. I know that there are so many benefits and I love the bonding feeling— when she was first born my MIL took her out of my arms constantly and would hold her for three hours or more, so in the beginning I had so much trouble bonding and sometimes it didn’t even feel like she was mine. So basically- both of these things are making me feel like an awful mom and I don’t know which to focus on or what to do. Do I abandon wanting to lose weight and focus all my energy and effort on getting my milk supply up and breastfeeding, so that she can get all the health benefits and so that I can feel better about being scared by the pediatrician and feed her the way I wanted to in the first place? Or do I focus all my energy and effort onto losing weight and just continue formula-feeding her, even though I would feel incredibly guilty like I was putting my happiness before my daughter’s health?
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0240 -->
> **FAIL:** Combo-feeding AND breastfeeding alone.  
> ***why:*** Milk came in full force and baby latched great from the start, BUT I had to pump from the moment I woke up in the ICU. I was determined to BF, open to supplementing with formula, but regardless pumping was absolutely necessary to establish supply and feed my child during PP. I couldn't produce reliably if I didn't pump, but I would suffer clogged ducts and inflammation if I didn't. If I wanted to quit breastfeeding altogether I would have had to medicate to end milk production, thus risk clogs and side-effects along the way, *and it was like this since day 2.* I didn't realise that I could end up in a situation where the choice to breastfeed meant I had to baby my boobs 24/7 OR cut production entirely, and that either choice would mean some level of suffering.  
> **FIX:** Pumping for 15-20m every 2 hours during the day, and 15-20m once during my 7h sleep window (I could keep sleeping into the day but then I'd pump every 2h again). This was necessary from the moment I was conscious. I should add that, while this was hard af, it got easier with time and I am ultimately happy that I committed.
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu19-0064 -->

### 21. An available sleep shift, existing treatment and a supportive household do not guarantee sleep or prevent postpartum distress.

**Findings location:** §2, §6 and §10’s first-weeks plan; extend §17.4. **Relationship:** Confirms and extends.

**Current conclusion:** “Make the first two weeks a full section: feeding, sleep, recovery supplies, the partner's shifts, visitors.”

**What should change / remain:** Add inability to sleep during protected time, immediate panic, intrusive harm thoughts without a stated wish to act, rage, guilt and missed care from exhaustion. The thirteen-day medication account is within the first two weeks; “six months” is its planned treatment duration. Keep help, medication experiences, later improvement and much easier later postpartum periods visible. Do not infer a diagnosis or treat support as a guarantee.

**Evidence:** 36 quote records assigned here for navigation; 23 full fields below. These groupings are not mutually exclusive experiences or counts of people.

<!-- quote:pbu01-0019 -->
> With my first, I had severe postpartum anxiety (and some depression). I couldn’t leave the house. I was consumed by fear of SIDS. I couldn’t switch my brain off and I genuinely didn’t know if I’d ever feel like myself again.

I thought she was cute but she didn’t feel like she was mine, a month after she was born I found out her father was cheating and it completely broke me, after everything I done, I obviously left him and had very little to do with her and eventually stopped seeing her completely, I had a pretty bad break down and was diagnosed with severe postpartum depression, obviously now I love my daughter more than anything thing in the world, I would die for her but I can’t even look at baby photos of her without crying thinking to myself why didn’t I love you the way a mother was ment to love her baby, my fiancé tells me everything will be different this time, and he’s here for me every step of the way and I’m not alone doing it this time, but the two biggest things in my mind is what if this time I do feel the love and then have the guilt eating at me about how I felt about my daughter or what if I don’t feel anything again and hate myself for it…and it’s the cycle of feeling like there’s something wrong with me, don’t get me wrong I do want another baby but I feel torn on the fact either way I’m going to be mad at myself…has anyone else gone through something like this and can maybe offer me some advice or  tell me what happened when it came to having another baby 
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0064 -->
> I was 4 days postpartum with my second baby when I experienced my first major panic attack. I genuinely thought I was having a heart attack, so I went to the ER later that evening. Thankfully, everything came back normal, and my OB diagnosed me with postpartum anxiety and prescribed Zoloft.
> 
> It’s been 9 days since then. My sleep has actually gotten better, but I experience terrible anxiety (honestly more anxiety than before but no panic attacks thank God!) throughout the day. I also spoke with a psychiatrist, who explained that I likely experienced postpartum blues after my first baby and am now dealing with postpartum anxiety after my second. She recommended continuing Zoloft for 6 months and then gradually tapering off.
> 
> I’ve been researching what tapering off Zoloft looks like, and honestly, some of what I’ve read has made me really anxious about the process itself.
> 
> I’m posting here because I could really use some reassurance from people who have been through this. I’d love to hear from anyone who has successfully tapered off Zoloft and had heightened anxiety in beginningof getting on it, was it as difficult as it sounds online? Did things evetually get better?
> 
> I’m hoping to hear that it’s manageable, that it gets easier, and most importantly, that it will all be worth it in the end. 
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0109 -->
> I had my first daughter in June of 2022. Birth trauma, NICU stay, horrible PPD/A, OCD, and intrusive thoughts. It became so bad that 17 months PP I admitted myself into a mental health hospital for a week. 
> 
> We started trying for baby number 2 last February. Has a miscarriage and got pregnant again in October. I didn’t enjoy my first pregnancy, but I didn’t hate it either. This last time…BLISS. Except for being high risk, having to deliver 3 weeks early, and having a c section due to transverse lie, it was a wonderful experience. The c section wasn’t that bad. I’m able to breastfeed without supplementing this time and I love it so much. My baby sleeps so well compared to her older sister at this age. I’m not in a fog. I can actually breathe and see my baby as mine and not a stranger. 
> 
> I LOVE my oldest. She’s so funny and very smart. Her imagination brings me such joy. It took a long time to bond with her. I have few memories of her first year due to the antidepressant I had been on for 8 years. Very foggy.
> 
> My eyes feel so open this time and I haven’t felt depressed or anxious since before I got pregnant last October. I’m literally the happiest I’ve been in YEARS. 
> 
> My mom has told me that she feels I am replacing my oldest with my youngest. She asked what the difference was. Gee, I’m not in the deep darks this time. This whole experience has been better and healthier. It’s making me feel guilty. I absolutely miss time with just my oldest. It’s hard not giving her all my attention. 
> 
> I don’t want people to think I love my youngest more or that I’m favoring her. I feel I’m just trying to soak it all up and enjoy everything because I didn’t and couldn’t with my oldest. I’m also done having babies. 
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0115 -->
> She only slept in max 2hr stretches from birth but over the past two weeks, max 1hr stretch and as of the past two nights, she’s barely slept at all. Shes also been feeding (breastfed) pretty much on the hour, every hour.

The house is quite small so I can hear her scream which sets me off in a panic - my husband isn’t as good at consoling her and I can hear him getting frustrated. I have slept on average 1-2 hours a day for two weeks now and I actually want my body to just collapse so I might be able to sleep. 
>

My daughter was (and still is) a good, easy baby. My husband was supportive. I was able to sleep as much as I want. I had a massive village of people willing to take my kiddo whenever I needed it. Finances were secure. Everything was okay. It really was. 
> 
> But it progressively wasn’t. And I thought I knew better because I already had depression. These sad thoughts of not feeling loved or connected or worthy were just that. My normal depression. I’m medicated. I’m in therapy.
<!-- /quote -->

**Further supporting IDs:** [pbu01-0111], [pbu01-0112], [pbu01-0161], [pbu01-0173], [pbu01-0394], [pbu18-0061], [pbu18-0069], [pbu18-0070], [pbu18-0110], [pbu19-0063], [pbu19-0065], [pbu19-0102], [pbu19-0116].

### 22. The other side includes capable helpers, frightened fathers, their own trauma and distress, and pregnant parents who harm or overwhelm them.

**Findings location:** §5 and the limit on partner-format claims in §17.1. **Relationship:** Extends.

**Current conclusion:** “From his side, he wants to help and doesn't know how”

**What should change / remain:** Separate willingness, capacity, behavior and safety. A partner can do household work yet struggle with emotional support; his anxiety can prevent outings or newborn care; a father can experience a birth as seeing his child die. One pregnant author takes responsibility for shoving her husband and throwing a cup. Do not merge a father’s reported intrusive thoughts with an author’s admitted violence, or assume every relationship problem is lack of information. Include later improvement and reliable support.

**Evidence:** 59 quote records assigned here for navigation; 28 full fields below. These groupings are not mutually exclusive experiences or counts of people.

He makes sure I eat, compliments what I've cleaned, hes more gentle.

I entertain the baby, put him to sleep, do the laundry, fold it and put it away, cook all the meals, go grocery shopping, pick up medicine, go to doc appointments, put gas in the car, etc. I’ve expressed to my husband that we should go grocery shopping with the baby to get out of the house but my husbands response is always “yeah yeah we’ll see” and I know that means no. <!-- quote:pbu18-0120 -->
> My wife has a rough time, two months post-partum. Exhaustion, hormones, healing body - she also has challenges with breast feeding and she struggles with anxiety. We also have a 4 years old. It is TOUGH, I know, it would be for anyone.
> 
> I'm doing my best: I'm present, I step up, I initiate, I'm doing household, I'm doing kids - there is nothing (apart from breastfeeding and shopping for clothes) that I won't do, by myself and reliably. I work mostly from home, with a flexible schedule which I'm already stretching to to its limits. She's on long term maternity leave. We are financially OK. We get some help from our parents. I'm not perfect but I don't think I have anything to be ashamed of, and in any case, I am in it neck deep, all day, every day.
> 
> The thing is, I feel that if I give her the slightest justification for it, she explodes over me with stress and blame. Sometimes I do make mistakes (but who doesn't), but nothing that would justify the response. Other times, I really think it is totally unjustified: I ask a question I was not supposed to ask, or she wanted to me to ask her something which I didn't - these kinds of things. The results is: all her stress, exhaustion, anxiety, thrown at me, unfiltered, loud, hurtful.
> 
> The problem is, this behavior is the single most emotionally draining factor for me of all the things that are going on. It doesn't happen every day but it fucking wrecks me when it does. I don't know to what extent is this normal, I may be more sensitive than average. I am emotionally drained and exhausted by this. I am supposed to be the pillar, the source emotional stability, for her, and for our older kid, but her dumping all her shit on me wears me down so fast. It's like the drowning person who you try to save and by clinging on to you they (unintentionally) drown you too. The fact that our children witness this, see these patterns of coping, wrecks me even more emotionally. I want them (and myself) to feel emotionally safe and learn healthy ways to cope with hard times.
> 
> When she's fine, she does acknowledge the issue but not the extent of it. She reached out to her doctor but they won't prescribe anti-anxiety meds because she's breastfeeding (she used to take some before pregnancy). I offered her a plethora of things: I can take our newborn for 3-4 hours at a time, she can go to have a massage, spa, have a coffee with a friend, or I can take the kid and she can stay at home alone - whatever (she won't have it). She doesn't want to do talk therapy, she's fed up with it. I don't think she's sharing much of her burden with her friends either (and she doesn't have a good relationship with her mom). I'm the only one around, and I'm the one to absorb it all - and I'm also tired on my own right.

<!-- quote:pbu18-0252 -->
> I’m currently 27 weeks pregnant and I’ve noticed a side of myself that honestly scares me a little.
> 
> Before pregnancy, I was a pretty patient person. My husband and I have been together for 7 years. We’ve had arguments like any couple, but I never had urges to hit him or physically react when I was annoyed.
> 
> Since becoming pregnant, I feel like my irritability has gone through the roof. It’s not even just my husband. I find myself getting annoyed with coworkers, family members, and random strangers, much faster than I used to. I literally want to fight everyone. 
> 
> The reason I’m posting is because I recently had an argument with my husband that really shocked me.  
> We were trying to meet up with my sister and her family to watch fireworks. They were following us in their car, it was dark, and we ended up getting separated because my husband didn’t have a clear address and wasn’t really communicating what he was doing. I was trying to coordinate with my sister on the phone while also figuring out where we were going.
> 
> The more stressed I got, the more frustrated I became because I felt like every time I asked my husband a question, he would either get defensive or answer with another question instead of just telling me the plan.  
> What scared me was how fast everything escalated.  
> I went from mildly annoyed to very angry in probably 10 minutes, and then from angry to absolutely raging in what felt like seconds.
> 
> I ended up screaming at him at the top of my lungs. I shoved his shoulder and tossed my Stanley cup into his lap. The second it happened I felt awful because that is not who I am. I apologized afterward and he accepted my apology, but I’ve been thinking about it ever since.
> 
> The thing is, when I look back, this isn’t the first time during pregnancy that I’ve noticed more aggressive impulses. I’ve caught myself having thoughts like wanting to smack someone’s arm when they’re being annoying or wanting to physically react instead of just rolling my eyes and moving on. I don’t remember ever feeling this way before pregnancy.
> 
> I’m not trying to excuse my behavior. I know what I did was wrong and I take responsibility for it.
> 
> I’m just wondering if anyone else experienced a dramatic increase in irritability, anger, or aggressive impulses during pregnancy that felt completely out of character for them.  
> Did it get better after delivery? Did you talk to your OB about it?
<!-- /quote -->

### 23. Family help can be a lifeline, an obligation, an unsafe dependency or a way to claim access and control.

**Findings location:** §§3, 5–6, 9 and §17.4. **Relationship:** Extends.

**Current conclusion:** “Helpers vs visitors is the distinction she draws”

**What should change / remain:** Add abuse histories, housing dependence, inappropriate sexual comments, threatened access, unwanted disclosure and financial gifts used as ammunition. Some need fewer intrusions; some need their family to show up at all; others protect relatives who are grieving and lose their own opportunity to celebrate. Announcements, registries and showers can be tied to fear of loss, estrangement and privacy rather than ordinary etiquette alone.

**Evidence:** 93 quote records assigned here for navigation; 27 full fields below. These groupings are not mutually exclusive experiences or counts of people.

Not once he truly held her to accountable, he would react and then he would forget what happened. I told him I would never let her have unsupervised time with him, bathe him or change his diaper. Also, I feel uncomfortable bringing my child around with me holding him. We agree on it, he tries to challenge the rules. He will be like if you need to go to washroom, let her watch him (we currently live with her) or bring him out so she can see him. He would say “that’s her grandchild”, like she has some authority over him. I even bought it up that I will extending the boundaries for my daughter, I won’t be changing my mind about it either.
> 
> His mom was disrespectful and she was overstepping my boundaries during my 1st pregnancy and postpartum period. That’s why I decided not to involve her this pregnancy, she can know about it. But she needs to keep her distance from me. 
> 
> Am I overreacting? Or are my feelings invalid?
> 
> More info: my boyfriend and I currently looking place, so we can move out.
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0063 -->
> We can afford to live on our own, it’s not easy to find a place where we lived. The housing crisis is awful here. We been looking for over 1 year now. Did you think we ask for this?
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0077 -->
> Last week I messaged her and said can I ask if I’ve done something wrong? You feel very distant, if I have done something please tell me so we can resolve… and she just said no, I just wanted to give you guys space in the first few months of pregnancy. It’s a weird thing for her to say, we don’t need space, I need my sister. I replied saying that and she just didn’t reply again. 
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0176 -->
> I have a friend who’s gone through a couple miscarriages and she’s currently pregnant and doctors keep telling her that her pregnancy might not be viable.she had a scan this week and there’s a sak, which is great news. I went through a stillbirth one year ago. (And for some reason, I think that it’s okay to compare my journey to her) 
> 
> I check in on her at least 1-2 times per week. I’m going through my text messages and I’m realizing that I keep comparing my stillbirth to her current pregnancy and offering advice when I should be supporting her. It’s no longer about me and my stillbirth. I’m unsure of what to say and how to respond when she gives me updates weekly. Please help, she’s only 6 weeks pregnant. How should I respond and help moving forward? I need to stop comparing and bringing up what I did during my pregnancy and still birth. It’s not the same. 
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0208 -->
> I found out I was pregnant about two weeks ago. My husband and I have been trying for our third baby and my assumption was that he seemed happy and on board with baby #3. It was actually him who brought it up when we got over some health hurdles with a family member … he said “maybe we should start trying again.” We had always planned to have 3 kids. 
> 
> So, after a few months of trying, I am now pregnant. Since I found out, I’ve mentioned a few times that I’m excited for the baby or let him know 3-4 times that I feel a bit nauseous when I get waves of it. Sometimes I also feel tired as I have a 2-year-old and a 5-year-old. My husband is a physician and works long hours and I stay home with the kids. 
> 
> Last night, he told me he doesn’t want to talk about the pregnancy until I reach 2nd trimester so we know it’s real, because I suffered a late 1st trimester miscarriage between my first 2 kids. 
> 
> I was kind of hurt by this and I just now feel so isolated in this pregnancy because I feel I need to be silent about it with him. Is this acceptable or is this selfish on his part? Should I remain quiet about everything? 
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0260 -->
> I don’t know what to tag this but I’ve been having horrible anxiety about my baby. I don’t know the gender yet— and I was saying I am so elated to have a boy or girl. No preference. Now im scared to have a girl. Not because I don’t want one but bc im scared for her. 
> 
> This came on because I’m visiting my nana and her husband is weird. I’ve known him since I was 8 years old and he consistently makes weird sexual comments to me. Before I got pregnant he said to me “when is your husband gonna get you pregnant? If he doesn’t soon I might have to step in.” And now that I am he’s getting very very close to me, trying to rub his feet on my feet, he will literally stand so close to me and lightly put his foot on top of my foot. It’s weird. And I saw his search engine once while trying to help him with a phone issue- the man loves porn and has a foot fetish. He’s told after folding my laundry which I told him not to do that he only sniffed a few pairs of my panties. All as “jokes”. 
> 
> These pregnancy hormones are shifting something in me and I’m not able to deal with him anymore. I’m scared and pissed and I don’t want to be around him. 
> 
> I started thinking about all the women who have been sexually assaulted, raped, and abused by men. I sobbed in church and had to walk out. I’m scared my baby is a girl. I feel like I won’t be able to protect her as much or as well as I can protect a baby boy. As a woman, I have been sexually assaulted, harassed, raped, coerced, and manipulated, and I’m horrified for my baby girl because I know a lot of women can relate. Too many women. Id venture to say 98% of women. I will love my baby so much if she is a girl but I’m scared for her. And I used to think the “man or bear” thing was dramatic— but I get it now that my life isn’t just about me. 
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0264 -->
> Primarily has do with trauma. My own specifically, what my sister went through, and some of my female friends growing up went through, and what I had to powerlessly watch as my stepdaughter endured her own trauma entirely outside of mine and her father's control.
> 
> It doesnt stop. Its constant, endless, it doesnt go away with age. It evolves.
> 
> I feel like women are born targets for great malice. I didnt want a daughter because I feel like im dragging her into the inferno with me. 
> 
> I have had men refer to my pregnancy as "parasitic" and even jested about birthing a "maneater". As if im carrying around a xenomorph and not a baby.
> 
> I wanted a son because I feel like maybe he could have better experiences and a different out look. Be raised to not be such an asshile.
> 
> Instead, im going to have to raise someone who needs to understand that there are people out there that want to hurt her just because she exists as a she. And she will have to learn to recognize that or perhaps face fatal happenings at the hand of malicious persons.  
> 
> I don't want her to have to experience even part of what I had to endure existing as a female.
> 
> When I saw her reveal on the monitor, I felt terror. 
> 
> In my head I was like, f\*ck this going to suck for the both of us.
> 
> So, its not like I really really wanted a son. I just wanted a boy because I feel like it would have been safer for my kid if they were born male.
> 
> Im 22 weeks pregnant, im scared. I hate being pregnant and all I can think about is the bullshit I had to go through and am terrified that she  experience the same things.
> 
> Ive been crying on and off for two days over it. Because im scared I have damned some one.
> 
> My husband is understanding and sympathetic. I have talked and cried at him about this for hours already. He assured me that my experiences wont be repeated, different parents, different philosophies, different religions, different regions.
> 
> I know logically, hes correct. She'll have entirely different life than anyone I met and from either of us as her parents.
> 
> Im not mourning a boy. Im mourning the girl... with what her sister has experienced, what I experienced, what my sister had experienced, Im legitimately scared and traumatized. 
> 
> The whole thing makes me disassociate from my body. I cant just fade away when shes finally here. No one else will be able to teach her how to defend herself from these evils.
> 
> I wish I didn't have to. I remember the "beware" conversations I also had with my stepdaughter over her life and it still wasn't enough to keep her safe from traumatizing harm...
> 
> So many bad things happen to little girls. 
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0313 -->
> Unexpectedly I called my mom about an hour after I found out and she was completely okay with it. Now I’m breathing easy. Now I’m actually excited. I wish it wasn’t like that, I’ve always wanted to be a mother. I’ve always had a maternal extinct. Just sad my first thought was “what will my family think” not what about me, and my life, with my fiancé and baby.
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0361 -->
> On to the stress, I have no support system at all really. Most of the family I was close to have passed and those still around were pretty abusive to me and struggle with addiction. I’ve accepted that I can’t change/help them (shoutout to therapy) and with that I’m absolutely not going to bring a baby around them. I was no contact with them for several months prior to becoming pregnant and I was happy with that. However since announcing they somehow found out (I’m not entirely sure how given the modified privacy settings on all my socials) and have been desperately messaging me trying to make contact. Another family member whom I was close with and supported through her pregnancies begged to move in with us with her three kids (including an infant) due to running from legal issues. Even if our house was big enough given the reason it wasn’t an option. She decided to block me afterward. While I continue to ignore them I’d be lying if I said it didn’t stress me out, I’m so scared of how they’ll act once my baby is here or how aggressive they’re attempts may get. And on some level on the other hand sometimes I start feeling so low I almost want to contact them just so I don’t feel so lonely. I know that’s selfish and wouldn’t be helpful in the long run so I stop myself. 
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu18-0362 -->
> As of now with my fiancés family they just leave me on read 75% of the time. No one has come to visit except once during the entire pregnancy with no invitations to visit either. On the occasional time I do get a message they just ask about the baby and tell me how they “can’t wait for December!!”. We made the registry and nobody has bought anything at all, which would be fine if they didn’t beg us to make one. I fear it comes off entitled but honestly I just hate feeling like I’m getting my hopes up for stuff that isn’t going to happen. I found out recently though they’ve been going shopping together a ton and getting stuff of their own theming despite knowing ours, I honestly don’t know what all it is because they don’t communicate with my fiancé or me so I’m scared we’re gonna end up with double of things that can’t be used. Again I don’t want to come off entitled I just really wish I could at least know what’s going on. I ended up just not having a baby shower due to feeling depressed and knowing I wouldn’t have anyone there to even support me, plus financially with how much we still need to buy for the baby it didn’t seem worth it. 
> 
> At this point I just feel so alone as I have no family of my own and I feel like I’m being treated like an incubator at best by my fiancés family. I feel kind of betrayed as well due to the spreading of info about the baby without my consent. At this point I don’t know if I even want anyone around much after my baby is born given how distant everyone has been during the whole pregnancy. At the same time idk how healthy that is. I feel so stressed with making sure we have everything and depressed with being alone that I just don’t know what to do anymore. I’m so excited for my baby but this entire pregnancy has felt so emotionally hard. My fiancé has tried to do everything he can to help and I’m immensely grateful for him but he works so often he can’t do everything. At this point I’m terrified I’m gonna have PPD and idk what to do about that either. It’s just feeling really hard.
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu19-0022 -->
> I’m thankful for her, but it’s like why can’t my family just support me. I mean genuinely support. Not just buy stuff off the registry just to use it as ammunition later (they love to throw up how they helped pay for this and this when i was in college and basically anything else they had to pay for while raising me), but genuinely support and ask about my mental and encourage me…
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu19-0075 -->
> I have had a miscarriage last year and it destroyed me and still affects me to this day. I am now pregnant again. I love watching Tiktok videos about pregnancy and being a new boy mum, and they help me take my mind off my anxiety during this pregnancy. But, in a lot of the videos I watch, which are all positive videos, there's a couple comments like "my baby just died", "lost mine today", "I got pregnant again and lost it". I get people want to share their pain for support but it isn't the place!!! It's bang out of order in my opinion! This is mainly happening on happy videos where people are sharing their joys of pregnancy, reaching important milestones etc., and it just sends people like me into a spiral when I see those comments!
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu19-0150 -->
> Then my mom goes "just wait, you'll be so mad at her soon enough" and completely smashed it. And she does this a lot. A LOT of the moms in my friend group do. Like dang just let me enjoy the happy feelings?? Why does every positive thing have to come with a side of "just you wait, you idiot!"?? Especially from my MOM...like I AM YOUR CHILD, can you PLEASE stop telling me how terrible it was to be a mom?? 😅😅😅 Yeesh.
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu19-0173 -->
> My mom has also scheduled a nonrefundable cruise the week that my baby is due to be born because she is “so stressed out from my pregnancy” that she needs to just get away that week instead of being here for me, she had preemptively used PTO for that week to help me post partum (cooking, cleaning etc)
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu19-0295 -->
> Now it’s all flooding back in and she doesn’t really have anyone else besides me so I am doing my best to be here as much as I can be but I am crumbling apart. I have been having immense panic attacks and so much constant worry. I am not coping well with this. Usually when something is triggering like this I politely have to say I’m sorry I have to remove myself from this, but I know in this situation I shouldn’t and I won’t. I have no friends that can relate to me, they’re all men. I don’t have a village. So I’m here on reddit. My husband of course is comforting, but still. I just don’t know how to handle this all. Especially knowing every milestone I have, she should be having as well.
<!-- /quote -->

<!-- quote:pbu19-0309 -->

<!-- /quote -->
